<strong>pada-nakha-nīra-janita-jana-pāvana</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-vāmana-rūpa jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of a dwarf-brāhmaṇa, Vāmanadev! All glories to You! O wonderful dwarf, by Your massive steps You deceive King Bali, and by the Ganges water that has emanated from the toenails of Your lotus feet, You deliver all living beings within this world.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kṣatriya-rudhira-maye jagad-apagata-pāpaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>snapayasi payasi śamita-bhava-tāpam</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-bhṛgupati-rūpa jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of Bhṛgupati (Paraśurāma)! All glories to You! At Kurukṣetra You bathe the earth in the rivers of blood from the bodies of the demoniac kṣatriyas that You have slain. The sins of the world are washed away by You, and because of You people are relieved from the blazing fire of material existence.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vitarasi dikṣu raṇe dik-pati-kamanīyaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>daśa-mukha-mauli-baliṁ ramaṇīyam</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-rāma-śarīra jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of Rāmachandra! All glories to You! In the battle of Laṅkā You destroy the ten-headed demon Rāvaṇa and distribute his heads as a delightful offering to the presiding deities of the ten directions, headed by Indra. This action was long desired by all of them, who were much harassed by this monster.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vahasi vapuṣi viśade vasanaṁ jaladābhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>hala-hati-bhīti-milita-yamunābham</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-haladhara-rūpa jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of Balarām, the wielder of the plow! All glories to You! On Your brilliant white body You wear garments the colour of a fresh blue rain cloud. These garments are coloured like the beautiful dark hue of the River Yamunā, who feels great fear due to the striking of Your plowshare.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nindasi yajña-vidher ahaha śruti-jātaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>sadaya-hṛdaya darśita-paśu-ghātam</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-buddha-śarīra jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of Buddha! All glories to You! O Buddha of compassionate heart, you decry the slaughtering of poor animals performed according to the rules of Vedic sacrifice.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mlechchha-nivaha-nidhane kalayasi karavālaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>dhūmaketum iva kim api karālam</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-kalki-śarīra jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of Kalki! All glories to You! You appear like a comet and carry a terrifying sword for bringing about the annihilation of the wicked barbarian men at the end of the Kali-yuga.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="11">
<h5>11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-jayadeva-kaver idam uditam udāraṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śṛṇu sukha-daṁ śubha-daṁ bhava-sāram</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-daśa-vidha-rūpa jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed these ten different forms of incarnation! All glories to You! O readers, please hear this hymn of the poet Jayadeva, which is most excellent, an awarder of happiness, a bestower of auspiciousness, and the best thing in this dark world.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="12">
<h5>12</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vedān uddharate jaganti vahate bhū-golam udbibhrate</strong><br>
<strong>daityaṁ dārayate baliṁ chhalayate kṣatra-kṣayaṁ kurvate</strong><br>
<strong>paulastyaṁ jayate halaṁ kalayate kāruṇyam ātanvate</strong><br>
<strong>mlechchhān mūrchchhayate daśākṛti-kṛte kṛṣṇāya tubhyaṁ namaḥ</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Lord Kṛṣṇa, I offer my obeisances unto You, who appear in the forms of these ten incarnations. In the form of Matsya You rescue the Vedas, and as Kūrma You bear the Mandara Mountain on Your back. As Varāha You lift the earth with Your tusk, and in the form of Nṛsiṁha You tear open the chest of the demon Hiraṇyakaśipu. In the form of Vāmana You trick the demoniac king Bali by asking him for only three steps of land, and then You take away the whole universe from him by expanding Your steps. As Paraśurāma You slay all of the wicked kṣatriyas, and as Rāmachandra You conquer the rākṣasa king Rāvaṇa. In the form of Balarām You carry a plough with which You subdue the wicked and draw toward You the River Yamunā. As Lord Buddha You show compassion toward all the living beings suffering in this world, and at the end of the Kali-yuga You appear as Kalki to bewilder the mlechas (degraded low-class men).</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-namas-te-narasimhaya" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>Śrī Nṛsiṁha Praṇām <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>[Sanskrit]</h3>
<p class="verse"><strong>namas te narasiṁhāya</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prahlādāhlāda-dāyine</strong><br>
<strong>hiraṇyakaśipor vakṣaḥ-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śilā-ṭaṅka-nakhālaye</strong></p>
<p class="translation">I offer my obeisances to Lord Nṛsiṁha, who gives joy to Prahlād Mahārāj and whose nails are like chisels on the stonelike chest of the demon Hiraṇyakaśipu.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<p class="verse"><strong>ito nṛsiṁhaḥ parato nṛsiṁho</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yato yato yāmi tato nṛsiṁhaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>bahir nṛsiṁho hṛdaye nṛsiṁho</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nṛsiṁham ādiṁ śaraṇaṁ prapadye</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Nṛsiṁha is here and also there. Wherever I go Lord Nṛsiṁha is there. He is in the heart and is outside as well. I surrender to Lord Nṛsiṁha, the origin of all things and the supreme refuge.<br /><i>(Śrīla Vyasādeva)</i></p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<p class="verse"><strong>tava kara-kamala-vare nakham adbhuta-śṛṅgaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>dalita-hiraṇyakaśipu-tanu-bhṛṅgam</strong><br>
<strong>keśava dhṛta-narahari-rūpa jaya jagadīśa hare</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Keśava! O Lord of the universe! O Lord Hari, who have assumed the form of half-man, half-lion! All glories to You! Just as one can easily crush a wasp between one’s fingernails, so in the same way the body of the wasplike demon Hiraṇyakaśipu has been ripped apart by the wonderful pointed nails on Your beautiful lotus hands.<br /><i>(Śrīla Jayadev Goswāmī)</i></p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-nadiya-nagare-nitai-neche-neche-gay-re" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>Śrīman Mahāprabhura Śata-nāma <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>One Hundred Names of Śrīman Mahāprabhu</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<p class="verse"><strong>nadīyā-nagare nitāi neche neche gāya re</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Dancing and dancing throughout the town of Nadia, Nitāi sings!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jagannātha-suta mahāprabhu viśvambhara</strong><br>
<strong>māyāpura-śaśi navadvīpa-sudhākara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(1) O son of Jagannātha Miśra, (2) O great master,<br />(3) O maintainer of the world, (4) O moon of Māyāpur,<br />(5) O source of nectar for Nabadwīp!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śachī-suta gaurahari nimāi-sundara</strong><br>
<strong>rādhā-bhāva-kānti-āchchhādita naṭavara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(6) O son of Śachī, (7) O golden Hari, (8) O beautiful Nimāi,<br />(9) O best of dancers covered with the heart and halo of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nāmānanda chapala bālaka mātṛ-bhakta</strong><br>
<strong>brahmāṇḍa-vadana tarkī kautukānurakta</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(10) O He whose ecstasy is the Name, (11) O restless boy,<br />(12) O devotee of His mother, (13) O He in whose mouth rests the entire universe, (14) O expert in logic, (15) O He who is fond of play!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vidyārthi-uḍupa chaura-dvayera mohana</strong><br>
<strong>tairthika-sarvasva grāmya-bālikā-krīḍana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(16) O moon amongst the seekers of knowledge,<br />(17) O bewilderer of the two theives, (18) O the all-in-all of pilgrims,<br />(19) O He who plays with the village girls!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>lakṣmī-prati vara-dātā uddhata bālaka</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-śachīra pati-putra-śoka-nivāraka</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(20) O giver of a boon to Lakṣmī Priyā, (21) O mischevious boy,<br />(22) O remover of Śrī Śachī’s grief over her husband and son!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>lakṣmī-pati pūrva-deśa-sarva-kleśa-hara</strong><br>
<strong>digvijayi-darpa-hārī viṣṇu-priyeśvara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(23) O husband of Lakṣmī Priyā, (24) O remover of all suffering from East Bengal, (25) O remover of the pride of the champion scholar Keśava Kāśmīri, (26) O Lord of Viṣṇu Priyā!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ārya-dharma-pāla pitṛ-gayā-piṇḍa-dātā</strong><br>
<strong>purī-śiṣya madhvāchārya-sampradāya-pātā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(27) O preserver of Āryan dharma, (28) O offerer of piṇḍa to His ancestors at Gayā, (29) O disciple of Śrī Īśvara Purī, (30) O protector of the sampradāya established by Śrī Madhva Āchārya!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kṛṣṇa-nāmonmatta kṛṣṇa-tattva-adhyāpaka</strong><br>
<strong>nāma-saṅkīrtana-yuga-dharma-pravartaka</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(31) O He who is maddened by the Name of Kṛṣṇa, (32) O teacher of the science of Kṛṣṇa, (33) O inaugurator of the dharma for the age: congregational chanting of the Holy Name!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>advaita-bāndhava śrīnivāsa-gṛha-dhana</strong><br>
<strong>nityānanda-prāṇa gadādharera jīvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(34) O dear friend of Advaita, (35) O wealth of Śrīvās’ home,<br />(36) O heart of Nityānanda, (37) O life of Gadādhar!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>antardvīpa-śaśadhara sīmanta-vijaya</strong><br>
<strong>godruma-vihārī madhyadvīpa-līlāśraya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(38) O moon of Antardwīp, (39) O triumph of Sīmantadwīp,<br />(40) O reveller of Godrumadwīp,<br />(41) O shelter of the Pastimes in Madhyadwīp!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="11">
<h5>11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>koladvīpa-pati ṛtudvīpa-maheśvara</strong><br>
<strong>jahnu-modadruma-rudradvīpera īśvara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(42) O Lord of Koladwīp, (43) O great Lord of Ṛtudwīp,<br />(44–46) O Lord of Jahnudwīp, Modadrumadwīp, and Rudradwīp!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="12">
<h5>12</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>navakhaṇḍa-raṅganātha jāhnavī-jīvana</strong><br>
<strong>jagāi-mādhāi-ādi-durvṛtta-tāraṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(47) O playful Lord of Nabadwīp, (48) O life of Gaṅgā,<br />(49) O deliverer of Jagāi, Mādhāi, and other misbehaved persons!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="13">
<h5>13</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nagara-kīrtana-siṁha kājī-uddhāraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>śuddha-nāma-prachāraka bhaktārti-haraṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(50) O lion of the village kīrtans, (51) O saviour of the Kāzī,<br />(52) O propagator of the pure Name,<br />(53) O remover of the devotees’ distress!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="14">
<h5>14</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nārāyaṇī-kṛpā-sindhu jīvera niyantā</strong><br>
<strong>adhama-paḍuyā-daṇḍī bhakta-doṣa-hantā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(54) O ocean of mercy for Nārāyaṇī, (55) O controller of all souls,<br />(56) O punisher of the degraded students,<br />(57) O destroyer of the devotees’ faults!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="15">
<h5>15</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-kṛṣṇa-chaitanyachandra bhāratī-tāraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>parivrāja-śiromaṇi utkala-pāvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(58) O moon known as Śrī Kṛṣṇa Chaitanya, (59) O deliverer of Śrī Keśava Bhāratī, (60) O crown-jewel of the itinerants,<br />(61) O purifier of Odisha!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="16">
<h5>16</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ambuliṅga-bhuvaneśa-kapoteśa-pati</strong><br>
<strong>kṣīra-chora-gopāla-darśana-sukhī yatī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(62–64) O Lord of Ambuliṅga, Bhuvaneśvara, and Kapoteśvara,<br />(65) O He who is happy to see Kṣīra-chora Gopīnāth and Sākṣī Gopāl, (66) O ascetic!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="17">
<h5>17</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nirdaṇḍi-sannyāsī sārvabhauma-kṛpāmaya</strong><br>
<strong>svānanda-āsvādānandī sarva-sukhāśraya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(67) O sannyāsī without a daṇḍa, (68) O He who is merciful to Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭāchārya, (69) O He who is joyful in tasting the joy of Himself, (70) O origin of all happiness!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="18">
<h5>18</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>puraṭa-sundara vāsudeva-trāṇa-kartā</strong><br>
<strong>rāmānanda-sakhā bhaṭṭa-kula-kleśa-hartā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(71) O beautiful golden Lord, (72) O deliverer of (the leper) Vāsudev,<br />(73) O friend of Rāmānanda Rāy,<br />(74) O remover of hardship from the Bhaṭṭa family!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="19">
<h5>19</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bauddha-jaina-māyāvādi-kutarka-khaṇḍana</strong><br>
<strong>dakṣiṇa-pāvana bhakti-grantha-uddhāraṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(75–77) O refuter of the fallacious arguments of the Buddhists, Jains, and Māyāvādīs, (78) O purifier of the South,<br />(79) O recoverer of Śrī Brahma-saṁhitā and Śrī Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="20">
<h5>20</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ālāla-darśanānandī rathāgra-nartaka</strong><br>
<strong>gajapati-trāṇa devānanda-uddhāraka</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(80) O He who is joyful to see Ālālanāth, (81) O dancer in front of the chariot, (82) O deliverer of Mahārāj Pratāparudra,<br />(83) O saviour of Devānanda Paṇḍit!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="21">
<h5>21</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kuliyā-prakāśe duṣṭa-paḍuyāra trāṇa</strong><br>
<strong>rūpa-sanātana-bandhu sarva-jīva-prāṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(84) O deliverer of the impudent students during His appearance in Kuliyā, (85–86) O friend of Rūpa and Sanātan, (87) O life of all souls!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="22">
<h5>22</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vṛndāvanānanda-mūrti balabhadra-saṅgī</strong><br>
<strong>yavana-uddhārī bhaṭṭa-vallabhera raṅgī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(88) O embodiment of the joy of Vṛndāvan, (89) O companion of Balabhadra Bhaṭṭāchārya, (90) O deliverer of the foreigners,<br />(91) O He who is jocular with Vallabha Bhaṭṭa!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="23">
<h5>23</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kāśī-vāsi-sannyāsī-uddhārī prema-dātā</strong><br>
<strong>markaṭa-vairāgī-daṇḍī āchaṇḍāla-trātā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(92) O saviour of the sannyāsīs of Kāśī, (93) O giver of divine love,<br />(94) O punisher of the false monkey-like renunciants,<br />(95) O deliverer of even the outcasts!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="24">
<h5>24</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhaktera gauravakārī bhakta-prāṇa-dhana</strong><br>
<strong>haridāsa-raghunātha-svarūpa-jīvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(96) O He who takes pride in the devotees, (97) O life and wealth of the devotees, (98–100) O life of Haridās, Raghunāth, and Svarūp!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="25">
<h5>25</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nadiyā-nagare nitāi neche neche gāya re</strong><br>
<strong>bhakati-vinoda tā̐ra paḍe rāṅgā-pāya re</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Dancing and dancing throughout the town of Nadia, Nitāi sings, and Bhakti Vinod falls at His reddish feet.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-nagare-nagare-gora-gay" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>Śrī Kṛṣṇera Viṁśottara-śata-nāma <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>One Hundred and Twenty Names of Śrī Kṛṣṇa</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<p class="verse"><strong>nagare nagare gorā gāya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">From village to village, Śrī Gaurāṅga sings!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yaśomatī-stanya-pāyī śrī-nanda-nandana</strong><br>
<strong>indra-nīla-maṇi vraja-janera jīvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(1) O drinker of Mother Yaśodā’s breastmilk, (2) O son of Śrī Nanda,<br />(3) O sapphire jewel, (4) O life of the people of Vraja!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-gokula-niśācharī-pūtanā-ghātana</strong><br>
<strong>duṣṭa-tṛṇāvarta-hantā śakaṭa-bhañjana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(5) O killer of the witch Pūtanā in Śrī Gokula,<br />(6) O destroyer of the wicked Tṛṇāvarta, (7) O dispeller of Śakaṭa!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>navanīta-chora dadhi-haraṇa-kuśala</strong><br>
<strong>yamala-arjuna-bhañjī govinda gopāla</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(8) O butter thief, (9) O expert at stealing yoghurt, (10) O breaker of the two Arjuna trees, (11) O pleaser of the cows, (12) O cowherd boy!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>dāmodara vṛndāvana-go-vatsa-rākhāla</strong><br>
<strong>vatsāsurāntaka hari nija-jana-pāla</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(13) O He who is bound about the belly by a rope, (14) O keeper of the cows and calves of Vṛndāvan, (15) O destroyer of Vatsāsura,<br />(16) O He who takes away (the sins, miseries, and obstacles to devotion from His devotees), (17) O maintainer of His own!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>baka-śatru agha-hantā brahma-vimohana</strong><br>
<strong>dhenuka-nāśana kṛṣṇa kāliya-damana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(18) O enemy of Baka, (19) O slayer of Agha, (20) O bewilderer of Brahmā, (21) O destroyer of Dhenuka, (22) O He who attracts and satisfies all, (23) O subduer of Kāliya!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>pītāmbara śikhi-pichchha-dhārī veṇu-dhara</strong><br>
<strong>bhāṇḍīra-kānana-līla dāvānala-hara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(24) O He of yellow garments, (25) O He who wears a peacock feather, (26) O holder of the veṇu flute, (27) O He who performs Pastimes in the Bhāṇḍīra forest, (28) O extinguisher of the forest fire!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>naṭavara guhāchara śarata-vihārī</strong><br>
<strong>vallabhī-vallabha deva gopī-vastra-hārī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(29) O best of dancers, (30) O He who wanders in the caves,<br />(31) O reveller of the autumn, (32) O lover of the cowherd maidens,<br />(33) O playful one, (34) O stealer of the gopīs’ clothes!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yajña-patnī-gaṇa-prati karuṇāra sindhu</strong><br>
<strong>govardhana-dhṛk mādhava vraja-vāsī-bandhu</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(35) O ocean of mercy for the wives of the brāhmaṇas engaged in sacrifice, (36) O lifter of Govardhan, (37) O descendent of Madhu,<br />(38) O friends of the residents of Vraja!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>indra-darpa-hārī nanda-rakṣitā mukunda</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-gopī-vallabha rāsa-krīḍa pūrṇānanda</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(39) O remover of Indra’s pride, (40) O protector of Nanda,<br />(41) O giver of liberation, (42) O lover of the gopīs,<br />(43) O player of Rāsa, (44) O complete joy!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-rādhā-vallabha rādhā-mādhava sundara</strong><br>
<strong>lalitā-viśākhā-ādi-sakhī-prāṇeśvara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(45) O lover of Śrī Rādhā, (46) O sweetness of Rādhā, (47) O beautiful one, (48) O Lord of the hearts of Lalitā, Viśākhā, and the other sakhīs!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="11">
<h5>11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nava-jaladhara-kānti madana-mohana</strong><br>
<strong>vana-mālī smera-mukha gopī-prāṇa-dhana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(49) O He of the complexion of a fresh rain cloud,<br />(50) O bewilderer of Cupid, (51) O He who wears a forest garland,<br />(52) O He of smiling face, (53) O life and wealth of the gopīs!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="12">
<h5>12</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>tri-bhaṅgī muralī-dhara yāmuna-nāgara</strong><br>
<strong>rādhā-kuṇḍa-raṅga-netā rasera sāgara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(54) O thrice bent, (55) O holder of the muralī flute, (56) O paramour on the Yamunā, (57) O leader of the play at Rādhā Kuṇḍa,<br />(58) O ocean of rasa!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="13">
<h5>13</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>chandrāvalī-prāṇanātha kautukābhilāṣī</strong><br>
<strong>rādhā-māna-sulampaṭa milana-prayāsī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(59) O Lord of the heart of Chandrāvalī, (60) O desirer for play,<br />(61) O Lord, eager to overcome of Rādhā’s jealous anger,<br />(62) O seeker of union!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="14">
<h5>14</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mānasa-gaṅgāra dānī prasūna-taskara</strong><br>
<strong>gopī-saha-haṭhakārī vraja-vaneśvara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(63) O tax-collector at Mānasa Gaṅgā, (64) O blossom thief, (65) O He who is impetuous with the gopīs, (66) O Lord of the forest of Vraja!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="15">
<h5>15</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gokula-sampada gopa-duḥkha-nivāraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>durmada-damana bhakta-santāpa-haraṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(67) O wealth of Gokula, (68) O vanquisher of the cowherders’ sorrow,<br />(69) O subduer of pride, (70) O remover of the devotees’ distress!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="16">
<h5>16</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sudarśana-mochana śrī-śaṅkhachūḍāntaka</strong><br>
<strong>rāmānuja śyāmachā̐da muralī-vādaka</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(71) O liberator of (the Vidyādhara) Sudarśan, (72) O destroyer of Śrī Śaṅkhachūḍa, (73) O younger brother of Balarām, (74) O dark moon,<br />(75) O player of the muralī flute!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="17">
<h5>17</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gopī-gīta-śrotā madhusūdana murāri</strong><br>
<strong>ariṣṭa-ghātaka rādhā-kuṇḍādi-vihārī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(76) O hearer of the Gopī-gītā, (77) O destroyer of Madhu,<br />(78) O enemy of Mura, (79) O slayer of Ariṣṭa,<br />(80) O reveller at Rādhā Kuṇḍa and other places!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="18">
<h5>18</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vyomāntaka padma-netra keśī-nisūdana</strong><br>
<strong>raṅga-krīḍa kaṁsa-hantā malla-praharaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(81) O killer of Vyoma, (82) O lotus-eyed one, (83) O destroyer of Keśī, (84) O Lord of merry play, (85) O killer of Kaṁsa,<br />(86) O combatant of the wrestlers!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="19">
<h5>19</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vasudeva-suta vṛṣṇi-vaṁśa-kīrti-dhvaja</strong><br>
<strong>dīnanātha mathureśa devakī-garbhaja</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(87) O son of Vasudev, (88) O emblem of the Vṛṣṇi dynasty’s fame,<br />(89) O Lord of the poor, (90) O Lord of Mathurā,<br />(91) O son of Devakī!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="20">
<h5>20</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kubjā-kṛpāmaya viṣṇu śauri nārāyaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>dvārakeśa naraka-ghna śrī-yadu-nandana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(92) O He who is merciful to Kubjā, (93) O all-pervading one,<br />(94) O descendant of Śūrasena, (95) O shelter of all beings,<br />(96) O Lord of Dvārakā, (97) O destroyer of Naraka,<br />(98) O son of Yadu!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="21">
<h5>21</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-rukmiṇī-kānta satyā-pati sura-pāla</strong><br>
<strong>pāṇḍava-bāndhava śiśupālādira kāla</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(99) O husband of Rukmiṇī, (100) O husband of Satyābhāmā,<br />(101) O protector of the gods, (102) O dear friend of the Pāṇḍavas,<br />(103) O death of Śiśupāl and others!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="22">
<h5>22</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jagadīśa janārdana keśavārta-trāṇa</strong><br>
<strong>sarva-avatāra-bīja viśvera nidāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(104) O Lord of the universe, (105) O exciter of the people,<br />(106) O He of fine hair, (107) O deliverer from distress,<br />(108) O origin of all Avatārs, (109) O cause of the universe!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="23">
<h5>23</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>māyeśvara yogeśvara brahma-tejādhāra</strong><br>
<strong>sarvātmāra ātmā prabhu prakṛtira pāra</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(110) O Lord of Māyā, (111) O master of yoga,<br />(112) O source of the Brahman effulgence, (113) O soul of all souls,<br />(114) O master of all souls, (115) O far shore of material nature!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="24">
<h5>24</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>patita-pāvana jagannātha sarveśvara</strong><br>
<strong>vṛndāvanachandra sarva-rasera ākara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(116) O purifier of the fallen, (117) O Lord of the universe,<br />(118) O Lord of all, (119) O moon of Vṛndāvan,<br />(120) O source of all rasa!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="25">
<h5>25</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nagare nagare gorā gāya</strong><br>
<strong>bhakati-vinoda tachhu pāya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">From village to village, Śrī Gaurāṅga sings, and Bhakti Vinod falls at His feet.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-shri-hari-vasare-hari-kirtana-vidhana" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Hari-vāsara Gīti <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h4>by Śrīla Vṛndāvan Dās Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-hari-vāsare hari-kīrtana-vidhāna</strong><br>
<strong>nṛtya ārambhilā prabhu jagatera prāṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">On the holy day of Śrī Hari (Ekādaśī), Śrīman Mahāprabhu, the life and soul of the universe, began dancing.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>puṇyavanta śrīvāsa-aṅgane śubhārambha</strong><br>
<strong>uṭhila kīrtana-dhvani gopāla govinda</strong></p>
<p class="translation">With the auspicious beginning of the kīrtan in the courtyard of the fortunate Śrīvās, the sound of the chanting, ‘Gopāl!’ ‘Govinda!’ arose.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mṛdaṅga mandirā bāje śaṅkha karatāla</strong><br>
<strong>saṅkīrtana saṅge saba ha-ila miśāla</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The sounds of the mṛdaṅgas, cymbals, conchshells, and clapping hands all combined together in the saṅkīrtan.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>brahmāṇḍe uṭhila dhvani pūriyā ākāśa</strong><br>
<strong>chaudikera amaṅgala yāya saba nāśa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The sound of the saṅkīrtan arose and filled the sky, destroying inauspiciousness in all directions.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>chatur-dike śrī hari-maṅgala saṅkīrtana</strong><br>
<strong>madhye nāche jagannātha miśrera nandana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Mahāprabhu Viśvambhar, the son of Jagannātha Miśra, danced, surrounded by the auspicious Harināma-saṅkīrtan on all sides.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sabāra aṅgete śobhe śrī chandana-mālā</strong><br>
<strong>ānande nāchaye sabe ha-iye vibholā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Everyone was decorated with sandalwood paste and flower garlands, and danced, overwhelmed with joy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nijānande nāche mahāprabhu viśvambhara</strong><br>
<strong>charaṇera tāli śuni ati manohara</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Mahāprabhu Viśvambhar danced in the ecstasy of Himself. Hearing the rhythm of His feet was heart capturing.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhāvāveśe mālā nāhi rahaye galāya</strong><br>
<strong>chhiṇḍiyā paḍaye giyā bhakatera gāya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">While He was rapt in ecstasy, His flower garland could not remain around His neck. It broke and fell as the devotees sang.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yā̐ra nāmānande śiva vasana nā jāne</strong><br>
<strong>yā̐ra rase nāche śiva se nāche āpane</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In the ecstasy of whose Name, Śiva does not notice if he is clothed. In that ecstasy which causes Śiva to dance, that Supreme Lord Himself now dances.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10-11">
<h5>10-11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yā̐ra nāme vālmīki ha-ila tapodhana</strong><br>
<strong>yā̐ra nāme ajāmila pāila mochana</strong><br>
<strong></strong><br>
<strong>yā̐ra nāme śravane saṁsāra-bandha ghuche</strong><br>
<strong>hena prabhu avatari kali-yuge nāche</strong></p>
<p class="translation">By whose Name Vālmīki became a powerful sage, by whose Name Ajāmil attained liberation, by hearing whose Name the bondage of material existence is destroyed, that very Lord descends in the Age of Kali and dances.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="12-13">
<h5>12-13</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yā̐ra nāma la-i śuka nārada beḍāya</strong><br>
<strong>sahasra-vadana-prabhu yāra guṇa gāya</strong><br>
<strong></strong><br>
<strong>sarva-mahā-prāyaśchita ye prabhura nāma</strong><br>
<strong>se prabhu nāchaye dekhe yata bhāgyavān</strong></p>
<p class="translation">You whose Name Śukadev and Nārad take as they wander about, whose glories the thousand-mouthed Anantadev sings, whose Name is the supreme form of atonement, that Supreme Lord now dances and glances upon the fortunate souls.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="14">
<h5>14</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī kṛṣṇa-chaitanya nityānanda-chā̐da jāna</strong><br>
<strong>vṛndāvana dāsa tachhu pada-yuge gāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Embracing Śrī Kṛṣṇa Chaitanya and Nityānanda Prabhu as his life and soul, Vṛndāvan Dās sings the glories of Their lotus feet.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-shiuddha-bhakata-charana-renu" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śuddha-bhakata <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śuddha-bhakata-&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;charaṇa-reṇu</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhajana-anukūla</strong><br>
<strong>bhakata-sevā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;parama-siddhi</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prema-latikāra mūla</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Dust from the lotus feet of pure devotees is conducive to devotional service, while service to the Vaiṣṇavas is itself the supreme perfection and the root of the tender creeper of divine love.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mādhava-tithi&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhakti-jananī</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yatane pālana kari</strong><br>
<strong>kṛṣṇa-vasati&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vasati bali’</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;parama ādare vari</strong></p>
<p class="translation">I observe with great care the holy days like Ekādaśī and Janmāṣṭamī, for they are the mother of devotion. As my dwelling place I choose with the greatest reverence and love the transcendental abode of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gaura āmāra&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ye-saba sthāne</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;karala bhramaṇa raṅge</strong><br>
<strong>se-saba sthāna&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;heriba āmi</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;praṇayi-bhakata-saṅge</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All those places where my Lord Gaurasundar travelled for Pastimes I will visit in the company of loving devotees.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mṛdaṅga-vādya&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śunite mana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;avasara sadā yāche</strong><br>
<strong>gaura-vihita&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kīrtana śuni’</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ānande hṛdaya nāche</strong></p>
<p class="translation">My mind always begs for the opportunity to hear the music of the mṛdaṅga. Upon hearing the kind of kīrtan ordained by Lord Gaurachandra, my heart dances in ecstasy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yugala-mūrti&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhiyā mora</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;parama-ānanda haya</strong><br>
<strong>prasāda-sevā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;karite haya</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sakala prapañcha jaya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">By beholding the Deity forms of the Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, I feel the greatest joy. By honouring the Lord’s prasādam, I conquer over all worldly illusions.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ye-dina gṛhe&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhajana dekhi</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gṛhete goloka bhāya</strong><br>
<strong>charaṇa-sīdhu&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhiyā gaṅgā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sukha nā sīmā pāya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Goloka Vṛndāvan appears in my home whenever I see the worship and service of Lord Hari going on there. Upon seeing the Ganges, which is a river of nectar emanating from the lotus feet of the Lord, my happiness knows no bounds.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>tulasī dekhi’&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;juḍāya prāṇa</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;mādhava-toṣaṇī jāni’</strong><br>
<strong>gaura-priya&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śāka-sevane</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jīvana sārthaka māni</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The sight of the holy Tulasī tree soothes my soul, for I know she gives pleasure to Lord Kṛṣṇa. By honouring śāk (a green leafy vegetable preparation), a dear favourite of Lord Chaitanya, I consider life worthwhile.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhakativinoda&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṇa-bhajane</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;anukūla pāya yāhā</strong><br>
<strong>prati-divase&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;parama-sukhe</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svīkāra karaye tāhā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Whatever Bhakti Vinod obtains that is conducive for the service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, he accepts every day with the greatest of joy.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-chaudda-shiata-sata-shiake-masa-je-phalgun" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="89">
<h2>Śrī Nimāi Sundar’s Advent <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>from Śrī Chaitanya Charitāmṛta: Ādi-līla 13.89–124</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Goswāmī</h4>
<h5>89</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>chaudda-śata sāta-śake māsa ye phālguna</strong><br>
<strong>paurṇamāsīra sandhyā-kāle haile śubha-kṣaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Thus in the year 1407 of the Śaka Era (A.D. 1486), in the month of Phālguna (February-March), in the evening of the full-moon day, the desired auspicious moment arrived.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="90">
<h5>90</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>siṁha-rāśi, siṁha-lagna, uchcha graha-gaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>ṣaḍ-varga, aṣṭa-varga, sarva sulakṣaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(According to the Jyotir-veda, or Vedic astrology, the auspicious birth moment is described as follows:) The moon was in Leo (the figure of the lion in the zodiac), Leo was the ascendant, several planets were strongly positioned, and the ṣaḍ-varga and aṣṭa-varga showed all-auspicious influences.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="91">
<h5>91</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>a-kalaṅka gaurachandra dilā daraśana</strong><br>
<strong>sa-kalaṅka chandre āra kon prayojana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When the spotless moon of Gaurachandra became visible, what would be the need for a moon full of black marks on its body?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="92">
<h5>92</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>eta jāni’ rāhu kaila chandrera grahaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘hari’ nāme bhāse tri-bhuvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Considering this, Rāhu, the black planet, covered the full moon, and immediately vibrations of “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa! Hari!” inundated the three worlds.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="93">
<h5>93</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya jaya dhvani haila sakala bhuvana</strong><br>
<strong>chamatkāra haiyā loka bhāve mane mana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All people thus chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa Mahāmantra during the lunar eclipse – their minds struck with wonder.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="94">
<h5>94</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jagat bhariyā loka bale – ‘hari’ ‘hari’</strong><br>
<strong>sei-kṣaṇe gaurakṛṣṇa bhūme avatari</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When the whole world was thus chanting the Holy Name of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa in the form of Gaurahari advented Himself on the earth.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="95">
<h5>95</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>prasanna ha-ila saba jagatera mana</strong><br>
<strong>‘hari’ bali’ hinduke hāsya karaye yavana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The whole world was pleased. While the Hindus chanted the Holy Name of the Lord, the non-Hindus, especially the Muslims, jokingly imitated the words.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="96">
<h5>96</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>‘hari’ bali’ nārīgaṇa dei hulāhuli</strong><br>
<strong>svarge vādya-nṛtya kare deva kutūhalī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">While all the ladies vibrated the Holy Name of Hari on earth, in the heavenly planets dancing and music were going on, for the demigods were very curious and excited.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="97">
<h5>97</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>prasanna haila daśa dik, prasanna nadījala</strong><br>
<strong>sthāvara-jaṅgama haila ānande vihvala</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this atmosphere, all the ten directions became jubilant, as did the waves of the rivers. Moreover, all beings, moving and nonmoving, were overwhelmed with transcendental bliss.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="98">
<h5>98</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nadīyā-udayagiri,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pūrṇachandra gaurahari,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛpā kari’ ha-ila udaya</strong><br>
<strong>pāpa-tamaḥ haila nāśa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tri-jagatera ullāsa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jagabhari’ hari-dhvani haya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Thus by His causeless mercy the full moon, Gaurahari, rose in the district of Nadia, which is compared to Udayagiri, where the sun first becomes visible. His rising in the sky dissipated the darkness of sinful life, and thus the three worlds became joyful and chanted the Holy Name of the Lord.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="99">
<h5>99</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sei-kāle nijālaya,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;uṭhiyā advaita rāya,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nṛtya kare ānandita-mane</strong><br>
<strong>haridāse lañā saṅge,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;huṅkāra-kīrtana-raṅge</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kene nāche, keha nāhi jāne</strong></p>
<p class="translation">At that time Śrī Advaita Āchārya Prabhu, in His own house at Śāntipura, was dancing in a pleasing mood. Taking Haridās Ṭhākur with Him, He danced and loudly chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa. But why they were dancing, no one could understand.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="100">
<h5>100</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>dekhi’ uparāga hāsi’,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śīghra gaṅgā-ghāṭe āsi’</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ānande karila gaṅgā-snāna</strong><br>
<strong>pāñā uparāga-chhale,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āpanāra mano-bale,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;brāhmaṇere dila nānā dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Seeing the lunar eclipse and laughing, Advaita Āchārya and Haridās Ṭhākur immediately went to the bank of the Ganges and bathed in the river in great jubilation. Taking advantage of the occasion of the lunar eclipse, Advaita Āchārya, by His mental strength, distributed various types of charity to the brāhmaṇas.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="101">
<h5>101</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jagat ānandamaya,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi’ mane sa-vismaya,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ṭhāreṭhore kahe haridāsa</strong><br>
<strong>tomāra aichhana raṅga,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;mora mana parasanna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi – kichhu kārye āchhe bhāsa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When he saw that the whole world was jubilant, Haridās Ṭhākur, his mind astonished, directly and indirectly expressed himself to Advaita Āchārya: “Your dancing and distributing charity are very pleasing to me. I can understand that there is some special purpose in these actions.”</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="102">
<h5>102</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>āchāryaratna, śrīvāsa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;haila mane sukhollāsa</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yāi’ snāna kaila gaṅgā-jale</strong><br>
<strong>ānande vihvala mana,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kare hari-saṅkīrtana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nānā dāna kaila mano-bale</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Āchāryaratna (Chandraśekhara) and Śrīvās Ṭhākur were overwhelmed with joy, and immediately they went to the bank of the Ganges to bathe in her waters. Their minds full of happiness, they chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra and gave charity by mental strength.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="103">
<h5>103</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ei mata bhakta-tati,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yāṅra yei deśe sthiti,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tāhāṅ tāhāṅ pāñā mano-bale</strong><br>
<strong>nāche, kare saṅkīrtana,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ānande vihvala mana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dāna kare grahaṇera chhale</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this way all the devotees, wherever they were situated, in every city and every country, danced, performed saṅkīrtana, and gave charity by mental strength on the plea of the lunar eclipse, their minds overwhelmed with joy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="104">
<h5>104</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>brāhmaṇa-sajjana-nārī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nānā-dravye thālī bhari’</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āilā sabe yautuka la-iyā</strong><br>
<strong>yena kāṅchā-soṇā-dyuti,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi’ bālakera mūrti,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āśīrvāda kare sukha pāñā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All sorts of respectable brāhmaṇa gentlemen and ladies, carrying plates filled with various gifts, came with their presentations. Seeing the newborn child, whose form resembled natural glaring gold, all of them happily offered their blessings.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="105">
<h5>105</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sāvitrī, gaurī, sarasvatī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śachī, rambhā, arundhatī</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āra yata deva-nārīgaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>nānā-dravye pātra bhari’,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;brāhmaṇīra veśa dhari’,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āsi’ sabe kare daraśana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Dressing themselves as the wives of brāhmaṇas, all the celestial ladies, including the wives of Lord Brahmā, Lord Śiva, Lord Nṛsiṁha­deva, King Indra, and Vasiṣṭha Ṛṣi, along with Rambhā, a dancing girl of heaven, came there with varieties of gifts.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="106">
<h5>106</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>antarīkṣe deva-gaṇa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gandharva, siddha, chāraṇa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;stuti-nṛtya kare vādya-gīta</strong><br>
<strong>nartaka, vādaka, bhāṭa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;navadvīpe yāra nāṭa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sabe āsi’ nāche pāñā prīta</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In outer space all the demigods, including the inhabitants of Gandharvaloka, Siddhaloka, and Chāraṇaloka, offered their prayers and danced to the accompaniment of music, songs, and the beating of drums. Similarly, in Nabadwīp town all the professional dancers, musicians, and blessers gathered together, dancing in great jubilation.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="107">
<h5>107</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kebā āse kebā yāya,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kebā nāche kebā gāya,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sambhālite nāre kāra bola</strong><br>
<strong>khaṇḍileka duḥkha-śoka, pramoda-pūrita loka,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;miśra hailā ānande vihvala</strong></p>
<p class="translation">No one could understand who was coming and who was going, who was dancing and who was singing. Nor could they understand one another’s language. Yet all unhappiness and lamentation were immediately dissipated, and everyone became all-jubilant. Thus Jagannātha Miśra was also overwhelmed with joy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="108">
<h5>108</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>āchāryaratna, śrīvāsa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jagannātha-miśra-pāśa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āsi’ tāṅre kare sāvadhāna</strong><br>
<strong>karāila jātakarma,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ye āchhila vidhi-dharma,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tabe miśra kare nānā dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Chandraśekhar Āchārya and Śrīvās Ṭhākur both came to Jagannātha Miśra and drew his attention in various ways. They performed the ritualistic ceremonies prescribed at the time of birth according to religious principles. Jagannātha Miśra also gave varieties of charity.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="109">
<h5>109</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yautuka pāila yata,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghare vā āchhila kata,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saba dhana vipre dila dāna</strong><br>
<strong>yata nartaka, gāyana,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhāṭa, akiñchana jana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhana diyā kaila sabāra māna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Whatever riches Jagannātha Miśra collected in the form of gifts and presentations, and whatever he had in his house, he distributed among the brāhmaṇas, professional singers, dancers, bhāṭas, and the poor. He honored them all by giving them riches in charity.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="110">
<h5>110</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrīvāsera brāhmaṇī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nāma tāṅra ‘mālinī’,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āchāryaratnera patnī-saṅge</strong><br>
<strong>sindūra, haridrā, taila,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kha-i, kalā, nārikela,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;diyā pūje nārīgaṇa raṅge</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The wife of Śrīvās Ṭhākur, whose name was Mālinī, accompanied by the wife of Chandraśekhara (Āchāryaratna) and other ladies, came there in great happiness to worship the baby with paraphernalia such as vermilion, turmeric, oil, fused rice, bananas, and coconuts.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="111">
<h5>111</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>advaita-āchārya-bhāryā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jagat-pūjitā āryā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nāma tāṅra ‘sītā ṭhākurāṇī’</strong><br>
<strong>āchāryera ājñā pāñā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gela upahāra lañā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhite bālaka-śiromaṇi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">One day shortly after Lord Gaurahari was born, Advaita Āchārya’s wife, Sītā Devī, who is worshipable by the whole world, took her husband’s permission and went to see that topmost child with all kinds of gifts and presentations.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="112">
<h5>112</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>suvarṇera kaḍi-ba-uli,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rajatamudrā-pāśuli,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;suvarṇera aṅgada, kaṅkaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>du-bāhute divya śaṅkha,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rajatera malabaṅka,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarṇa-mudrāra nānā hāragaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">She brought different kinds of golden ornaments, including armlets, necklaces, anklets, and bangles for the hands.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="113">
<h5>113</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vyāghra-nakha hema-jaḍi,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kaṭi-paṭṭasūtra-ḍorī</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hasta-padera yata ābharaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>chitra-varṇa paṭṭa-sāḍī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;buni photo paṭṭapāḍī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarṇa-raupya-mudrā bahu-dhana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">There were also tiger nails set in gold, waist decorations of silk and lace, ornaments for the hands and legs, nicely printed silken saris, and a child’s garment, also made of silk. Many other riches, including gold and silver coins, were also presented to the child.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="114">
<h5>114</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>durvā, dhānya, gorochana,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;haridrā, kuṅkuma, chandana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;maṅgala-dravya pātra bhariyā</strong><br>
<strong>vastra-gupta dolā chaḍi’&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṅge lañā dāsī cheḍī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vastrālaṅkāra peṭāri bhariyā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Riding in a palanquin covered with cloth and accompanied by maidservants, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī came to the house of Jagannātha Miśra bringing with her many auspicious articles such as fresh grass, paddy, gorochana, turmeric, kumkum, and sandalwood. All these presentations filled a large basket.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="115">
<h5>115</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhakṣya, bhojya, upahāra,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṅge la-ila bahu bhāra,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śachī-gṛhe haila upanīta</strong><br>
<strong>dekhiyā bālaka-ṭhāma,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sākṣāt gokula-kāna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;varṇa-mātra dekhi viparīta</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī came to the house of Śachī Devī, bringing with her many kinds of eatables, dresses, and other gifts, she was aston­ished to see the newborn child, for she appreciated that except for a difference in colour, the child was directly Kṛṣṇa of Gokula Himself.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="116">
<h5>116</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sarva aṅga – sunirmāṇa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;suvarṇa-pratimā-bhāna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sarva aṅga – sulakṣaṇamaya</strong><br>
<strong>bālakera divya jyoti,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi’ pāila bahu prīti,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vātsalyete dravila hṛdaya</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Seeing the transcendental bodily effulgence of the child, each of His nicely constructed limbs full of auspicious signs and resembling a form of gold, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī was very pleased, and because of her maternal affection, she felt as if her heart were melting.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="117">
<h5>117</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>durvā, dhānya, dila śīrṣe,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kaila bahu āśīṣe,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chirajīvī hao dui bhāi</strong><br>
<strong>ḍākinī-śāṅkhinī haite,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śaṅkā upajila chite,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ḍare nāma thuila ‘nimāi’</strong></p>
<p class="translation">She blessed the newborn child by placing fresh grass and paddy on His head and saying, “May You be blessed with a long duration of life.” But being afraid of ghosts and witches, she gave the child the Name Nimāi.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="118">
<h5>118</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>putramātā-snānadine,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dila vastra vibhūṣaṇe,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;putra-saha miśrere sammāni’</strong><br>
<strong>śachī-miśrera pūjā lañā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;manete hariṣa hañā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghare āilā sītā ṭhākurāṇī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">On the day the mother and son bathed and left the maternity home, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī gave them all kinds of ornaments and garments and then also honored Jagannātha Miśra. Then Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī, being honored by mother Śachī Devī and Jagannātha Miśra, was greatly happy within her mind, and thus she returned home.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="119">
<h5>119</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>aichhe śachī-jagannātha,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;putra pāñā lakṣmīnātha,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pūrṇa ha-ila sakala vāñchhita</strong><br>
<strong>dhana-dhānye bhare ghara,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;lokamānya kalevara,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dine dine haya ānandita</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this way mother Śachī Devī and Jagannātha Miśra, having obtained a son who was the husband of the goddess of fortune, had all their desires fulfilled. Their house was always filled with riches and grains. As they saw the beloved body of their son, day after day their pleasure increased.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="120">
<h5>120</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>miśra – vaiṣṇava, śānta,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;alampaṭa, śuddha, dānta,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhana-bhoge nāhi abhimāna</strong><br>
<strong>putrera prabhāve yata,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhana āsi’ mile, tata,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;viṣṇu-prīte dvije dena dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Jagannātha Miśra was an ideal Vaiṣṇava. He was peaceful, restrained in sense gratification, pure, and controlled. Therefore he had no desire to enjoy material opulence. Whatever money came because of the influence of his transcendental son, he gave it in charity to the brāhmaṇas for the satisfaction of Viṣṇu.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="121">
<h5>121</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>lagna gaṇi’ harṣamati,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīlāmbara chakravartī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gupte kichhu kahila miśrere</strong><br>
<strong>mahāpuruṣera chihna,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;lagne aṅge bhinna bhinna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi, – ei tāribe saṁsāre</strong></p>
<p class="translation">After calculating the birth moment of Lord Gaurahari, Nīlāmbara Chakravartī privately said to Jagannātha Miśra that he saw all the different symptoms of a great personality in both the body and birth moment of the child. Thus he understood that in the future this child would deliver all the three worlds.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="122">
<h5>122</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>aichhe prabhu śachī-ghare,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛpāya kaila avatāre,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yei ihā karaye śravaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>gaura-prabhu dayāmaya,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tāṅre hayena sadaya,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sei pāya tāṅhāra charaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this way Mahāprabhu, out of His causeless mercy, made His advent in the house of Śachī Devī. Gaurahari is very merciful to anyone who hears this narration of His birth, and thus such a person attains the lotus feet of the Lord.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="123">
<h5>123</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>pāiyā mānuṣa janma,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ye nā śune gaura-guṇa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hena janma tāra vyartha haila</strong><br>
<strong>pāiyā amṛtadhunī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;piye viṣa-garta-pāni,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;janmiyā se kene nāhi maila</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Anyone who attains a human body but does not hear of the glories of Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, his birth becomes useless. Amṛtadhunī is a flowing river of the nectar of devotional service. If after getting a human body one drinks the water in a poison pit of material happiness instead of the water of such a river, it would be better for him not to have lived, but to have died long ago.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="124">
<h5>124</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-chaitanya-nityānanda,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āchārya advaitachandra,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarūpa-rūpa-raghunāthadāsa</strong><br>
<strong>iṅhā-sabāra śrī-charaṇa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śire vandi nija-dhana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;janma-līlā gāila kṛṣṇadāsa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Taking on my head as my own property the lotus feet of Śrī Chaitanya Mahāprabhu, Nityānanda Prabhu, Āchārya Advaitachandra, Svarūpa Dāmodara, Rūpa Goswāmī, and Raghunātha Dāsa Goswāmī, I, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Goswāmī, have thus described the Lord’s advent.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-gopinath-mama-nivedana-shiuno" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Nivedana <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Part One</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, mama nivedana śuna</strong><br>
<strong>viṣayī durjana&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sadā kāma-rata</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kichhu nāhi mora guṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, Lord of the gopīs, please hear my submission. I am a wicked materialist, always addicted to worldly desires, and no good qualities do I possess.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmāra bharasā tumi</strong><br>
<strong>tomāra charaṇe&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;la-inu śaraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tomāra kiṅkara āmi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You are my only hope, and therefore I have taken shelter at Your lotus feet. I am now Your eternal servant.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, kemane śodhibe more</strong><br>
<strong>nā jāni bhakati&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;karme jaḍa-mati</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;paḍechhi saṁsāra ghore</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, how will You purify me? I do not know what devotion is, and my materialistic mind is absorbed in fruitive work. I have fallen into this dark and perilous worldly existence.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, sakali tomāra māyā</strong><br>
<strong>nāhi mama bala&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jñāna sunirmala</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svādhīna nahe e kāyā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, everything here is Your illusory energy. I have no strength or transcendental knowledge, and this body of mine is not independent and free from the control of material nature.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, niyata charaṇe sthāna</strong><br>
<strong>māge e pāmara&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kā̐diyā kā̐diyā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;karahe karuṇā dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, this sinner, who is weeping and weeping, begs for an eternal place at Your divine feet. Please give him Your mercy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, tumi ta sakali pāra</strong><br>
<strong>durjane tārite&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tomāra śakati</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ke āchhe pāpīra āra</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You are able to do anything, and therefore You have the power to deliver all sinners. Who is there that is more of a sinner than myself?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, tumi kṛpā-pārāvāra</strong><br>
<strong>jīvera kāraṇe&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āsiyā prapañche</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;līlā kaile suvistāra</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You are the ocean of mercy. Having come into this phenomenal world, You expand Your divine Pastimes for the sake of the fallen souls.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmi ki doṣera doṣī</strong><br>
<strong>asura sakala&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pāila charaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vinoda thākila vasi’</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I am so sinful that although all the demons attained Your lotus feet, Bhakti Vinod has remained in worldly existence.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-gopinath-ghuchao-samsara-jvala" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Nivedana <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Part Two</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, ghuchāo saṁsāra-jvālā</strong><br>
<strong>avidyā-yātanā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āra nāhi sahe</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;janama-maraṇa-mālā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, please remove the torment of worldly existence. I can no longer tolerate the pain of ignorance and the repeated succession of rebirth and death.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmi ta kāmera dāsa</strong><br>
<strong>viṣaya-vāsanā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jāgichhe hṛdaye</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;phā̐dichhe karama phā̐sa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, indeed I am a servant of lust. Worldly desires are awakening in my heart, and thus the noose of fruitive work is beginning to tighten.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, kabe vā jāgiba āmi</strong><br>
<strong>kāma-rūpa ari&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dūre teyāgiba</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hṛdaye sphuribe tumi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, when will I wake up and abandon afar this enemy of lust, and when will You manifest Yourself in my heart?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmi ta tomāra jana</strong><br>
<strong>tomāre chhāḍiyā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṁsāra bhajinu</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhuliyā āpana-dhana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I am Your devotee, but having abandoned You and thus having forgotten my real treasure, I have worshipped this mundane world.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, tumi ta sakali jāna</strong><br>
<strong>āpanāra jane&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;daṇḍiyā ekhana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śrī-charaṇe deha sthāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You know everything. Now, having punished Your servant, please give him a place at Your lotus feet.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, ei ki vichāra tava</strong><br>
<strong>vimukha dekhiyā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chhāḍa nija-jane</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nā kara karuṇā-lava</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, is this Your judgement, that seeing me averse to You, You abandon Your servant and don’t bestow even a particle of mercy upon him?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmi ta mūrakha ati</strong><br>
<strong>kise bhāla haya&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kabhu nā bujhinu</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tāi hena mama gati</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I am certainly very foolish, and I have never known what is good for me. Therefore such is my condition.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, tumi ta paṇḍita-vara</strong><br>
<strong>mūḍhera maṅgala&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sadā anveṣibe</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;e dāse nā bhāva para</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You are indeed the wisest person. Please look for a way to bring about auspiciousness for this fool, and please do not consider this servant as an outsider.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-gopinath-amara-upaya-nai" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Nivedana <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Part Three</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, āmāra upāya nāi</strong><br>
<strong>tumi kṛpā kari’&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āmāre la-ile</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṁsāre uddhāra pāi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I have no means of success, but if You take me, having bestowed Your mercy upon me, then I will obtain deliverance from this world.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, paḍechhi māyāra phere</strong><br>
<strong>dhana, dārā, suta&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghirechhe āmāre</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kāmete rekhechhe jere</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I have fallen into the perils of material illusion. Wealth, wife, and sons have surrounded me, and lust has wasted me away.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, mana ye pāgala mora</strong><br>
<strong>nā māne śāsana&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sadā achetana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;viṣaye rayechhe bhora</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, my mind is crazy and does not care for any authority. It is always senseless and has remained in the dark pit of worldly affairs.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, hāra ye menechhi āmi</strong><br>
<strong>aneka yatana&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ha-ila viphala</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ekhana bharasā tumi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, I have accepted my defeat. All of my various endeavours were useless. Now You are the only hope.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, kemane ha-ibe gati</strong><br>
<strong>prabala indriya&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vaśī-bhūta mana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nā chhāḍe viṣaya-rati</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, how shall I make any advancement when my mind has come under the control of the powerful senses and does not abandon its attachment to materialism?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, hṛdaye vasiyā mora</strong><br>
<strong>manake śamiyā&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;laha nija-pāne</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghuchibe vipada ghora</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, after sitting down in the core of my heart and subduing my mind, please take me to You. In this way the horrible dangers of this world will disappear.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, anātha dekhiyā more</strong><br>
<strong>tumi hṛṣīkeśa&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hṛṣīka damiyā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tāra’ he saṁsṛti-ghore</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, You are Hṛṣīkeśa, the Lord of the senses. Seeing me so helpless, please control these senses of mine and deliver me from this dark and perilous worldly existence.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopīnāth, galāya legechhe phā̐sa</strong><br>
<strong>kṛpā-asi dhari’&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bandhana chhediyā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vinode karaha dāsa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Gopīnāth, the noose of materialism has become fixed around my neck. Taking up the sword of Your mercy and cutting this bondage, make this Bhakti Vinod Your humble servant.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<p class="verse">(he govinda! gopīnātha!)<br>
(ei bār āmāy dayā kara he govinda! gopīnātha!)<br>
(bahu janme aparādhī he govinda! gopīnātha!)<br>
(lakṣa lakṣa janam gela he govinda! gopīnātha!)<br>
(śrī-charaṇe śaraṇ nilām he govinda! gopīnātha!)<br>
(śrī-charaṇe sevā diyā he govinda! gopīnātha!)</p>
<p class="translation">(O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(This time, please be merciful to me, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(I have been an offender for many births, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(Millions of births have passed, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(I have taken shelter at Your holy feet, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(I have surrendered at Your feet, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)<br />(Please give me the service of Your feet, O Govinda! O Gopīnāth!)</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-hari-he-dayala-mora-jaya-radha-natha" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Dainya O Prapatti <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h4>unknown composer, from the Maṭh’s Śrī Gauḍīya Gitāñjali</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>hari he dayāla mora jaya rādhānātha</strong><br>
<strong>bāra bāra ei-bāra laha nija sātha</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Hari! O my merciful Lord! All glories to You, the Lord of Rādhā! Many times I have avoided You, but this time please take me as Your own.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bahu yoni bhrami’ nātha! la-inu śaraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>nija-guṇe kṛpā kara adhama tāraṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Lord, after wandering through many wombs, I have taken refuge in You. Be merciful and deliver this wretched soul by Your divine power.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jagata-kāraṇa tumi jagata-jīvana</strong><br>
<strong>tomā chhāḍā kāro nahi he rādhā-ramaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O lover of Rādhā, You are the cause of the universe and the life of the universe. Without You no one has anything.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhuvana-maṅgala tumi bhuvanera pati</strong><br>
<strong>tumi upekṣile nātha! ki ha-ibe gati</strong></p>
<p class="translation">You bring about auspiciousness for the world, and You are the master of all the worlds as well. O Lord, what will be my refuge if You neglect me?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhāviyā dekhinu ei jagata mājhāre</strong><br>
<strong>tomā vinā keha nāhi e dāse uddhāre</strong></p>
<p class="translation">I have considered that in this world there is no one except You who can deliver this fallen servant.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-jaya-radhe-jaya-krishna-jaya-vrindavan" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Vraja Dhām Mahimāmṛta <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>The Nectarean Glories of Śrī Vraja Dhām.</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadās Kavirāj Goswāmī</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya rādhe jaya kṛṣṇa jaya vṛndāvana</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-govinda gopīnātha madana-mohan</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and the divine forest of Vṛndāvan. All glories to the three presiding Deities of Vṛndāvan – Śrī Govinda, Gopīnāth, and Madana-Mohan.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śyāma-kuṇḍa rādhā-kuṇḍa giri-govardhana</strong><br>
<strong>kālindī yamunā jaya jaya mahāvana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Śyāma Kuṇḍa, Rādhā Kuṇḍa, Govardhan Hill, and the Yamunā River (Kālindī). All glories to the great forest known as Mahāvan, where Kṛṣṇa and Balarām displayed all of Their childhood Pastimes.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>keśī-ghāṭa vaṁśī-vaṭa dvādaśa-kānana</strong><br>
<strong>yā̐hā saba līlā kaila śrī-nanda-nandana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Keśī-ghāṭa, where Kṛṣṇa killed the Keśī demon. All glories to the Vaṁśī-vaṭa tree, where Kṛṣṇa attracted all the gopīs to come by playing His flute. Glories to all of the twelve forests of Vraja. At these places the son of Nanda, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, performed all of His Pastimes.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-nanda yaśodā jaya jaya gopa-gaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>śrīdāmādi jaya jaya dhenu-vatsa-gaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Kṛṣṇa’s divine father and mother, Nanda and Yaśodā. All glories to the cowherd boys headed by Śrīdāmā (the older brother of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī) and Anaṅga Mañjarī. All glories to the cows and calves of Vraja.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya vṛṣabhānu jaya kīrtidā sundarī</strong><br>
<strong>jaya paurṇamāsī jaya ābhīra-nāgarī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Rādhā’s divine father and mother, Vṛṣabhānu and the beautiful Kīrtidā. All glories to Paurṇamāsī, the mother of Sāndīpani Muni, grandmother of Madhumaṅgal and Nāndīmukhī, and beloved disciple of Devarṣi Nārada. All glories to the young cowherd maidens of Vraja.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya jaya gopeśvara vṛndāvana-mājha</strong><br>
<strong>jaya jaya kṛṣṇa-sakhā vaṭu dvija-rāja</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories, all glories to Gopeśvara Śiva, who resides in Vṛndāvan in order to protect the holy dhām. All glories, all glories to Kṛṣṇa’s funny brāhmaṇa friend, Madhumaṅgal.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya rāma-ghāṭa jaya rohiṇī-nandana</strong><br>
<strong>jaya jaya vṛndāvana-vāsī yata jana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to Rāma-ghāṭa, where Lord Balarām performed His Rāsa Dance. All glories to Lord Balarām, the son of Rohiṇī. All glories, all glories to all of the residents of Vṛndāvan.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya dvija-patnī jaya nāga-kanyā-gaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>bhaktite yā̐hārā pāila govinda-charaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to the wives of the proud Vedic brāhmaṇas. All glories to the wives of the Kālīya serpent. Through pure devotion they all obtained the lotus feet of Lord Govinda.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-rāsa-maṇḍala jaya jaya rādhā-śyāma</strong><br>
<strong>jaya jaya rāsa-līlā sarva-manorama</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories to the place where the Rāsa Dance of Śrī Kṛṣṇa was performed. All glories to Rādhā and Śyāma. All glories, all glories to the divine Rāsa Dance, which is the most beautiful of all of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Pastimes.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jaya jayojjvala-rasa sarva-rasa-sāra</strong><br>
<strong>parakīyā-bhāve yāhā vrajete prachāra</strong></p>
<p class="translation">All glories, all glories to the mellow of conjugal love, which is the most excellent of all rasas and is propagated in Vraja by Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the form of the divine parakīya-bhāva (paramour love).</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="11">
<h5>11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-jāhnavā-pāda-padma kariyā smaraṇa</strong><br>
<strong>dīna kṛṣṇa-dāsa kahe nāma-saṅkīrtana</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Remembering the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda’s consort, Śrī Jāhnavā Devī, this very fallen and lowly Kṛṣṇadās sings the saṅkīrtan of the Holy Name.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-jaya-yashoda-nandana-krishna-gopala-govinda" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Jaya Yaśodā Nandana Kṛṣṇa <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse">(jaya)<strong> yaśodā-nandana kṛṣṇa gopāla govinda</strong><br>
(jaya)<strong> madana-mohana hari ananta mukunda</strong></p>
